- FIX 2 (updater gates, Kotlin only): LiveUpdater.passesGates now runs (1) PackageManager parse + packageName/versionCode match, (2) NEW full ZIP CRC sweep — every entry read, ZipFile validates CRC, catches bit-flipped classes.dex BEFORE the installer, (3) NEW optional manifest sha256 enforcement (legacy manifests without the field still work); Manifest data class gained sha256, fetchManifest reads it; honest new Failed copy ("corrupted in transit" / "checksum mismatch")
- VERSION: committed defaults bumped to versionCode 4 / 0.1.3-native; pushed 5ff0669 → Android CI green (34349893104)
- PUBLISH: CI artifact app-release.apk (13,485,520 bytes) verified (versionCode 4 via real AXML chunk walk, versionName 0.1.3-native UTF-16LE in pool, v1+v2 present, zip clean, sha256 c52e6248…) → Release v0.1.3-native created via REST (id 385490556; NOTE create-release rejects short-sha target_commitish — 422 "not a valid tag", full sha required) + asset Pulse-v0.1.3-native.apk uploaded → download/Pulse.apk replaced + update-manifest.json now carries sha256 + points at the immutable release-asset URL → pushed 7f85853

Work Log:
- FORENSICS ON THE EXACT PUBLIC BYTES (this is the decisive new evidence): downloaded the live release asset (Pulse-v0.1.5-native.apk, 13,982,051 bytes) — sha256 df708296… == CDN manifest == repo download/Pulse.apk (byte-identical chain). ZIP: 164 entries, testzip clean. resources.arsc STORED + 4-byte aligned. Native libs: all 8 .so DEFLATED + unaligned — which is VALID because the binary AXML inside the APK proves android:extractNativeLibs=TRUE (AGP legacy packaging, correct for minSdk<23). AXML walk of the shipped manifest: versionCode=6, versionName=0.1.5-native, package=app.pulse.chat, minSdk=21, targetSdk=35 — all exactly as intended. v1 (CERT.SF/CERT.RSA) + v2/v3 block (magic "APK Sig Block 42" @ 13968751) present. Keystore: single blob (125adbf0…) since N4-a, never regenerated → no signature-drift class.
- CONCLUSION: the shipped APK is structurally valid for any stock Android 5.0-15 device; every in-app gate (magic, PackageManager parse, package+versionCode, ZIP CRC sweep, sha256) runs BEFORE the installer intent, so a device that reaches the system parse dialog had verified bytes. The failure therefore lives in the device-side install path we do not control: (a) corrupted manual/browser download that never passed our gates (GitHub asset downloads reset mid-transfer on congested networks — the raw CDN is proven reachable on the user's network because the manifest arrives), (b) OEM installer quirks with content:// handoffs, (c) foreign-signer refusal rendered as the generic parse error by some ROMs. Sandbox has a JRE only (no javac/jshell) → apksig cryptographic verification not run; AGP-signed blocks + zero CI post-processing (workflow verified: checkout→assembleRelease→upload only) keep signature validity near-certain.
- FIX WAVE (v0.1.6-native, versionCode 7) — make the app itself bulletproof and self-diagnosing:
  1) LiveUpdater.install rewritten: primary path = platform PackageInstaller session (SessionParams.MODE_FULL_INSTALL, openWrite+fsync+commit, PendingIntent broadcast status receiver, package-scoped action, FLAG_MUTABLE). The receiver surfaces the EXACT system verdict in the update UI: STATUS_PENDING_USER_ACTION → fires the confirm dialog; STATUS_SUCCESS → Idle; FAILURE_* mapped to honest text (BLOCKED → "blocked by the device (Play Protect or unknown-apps policy)", INVALID → "the system could not parse the update — tap to re-download", CONFLICT → "uninstall the old Pulse first", STORAGE, INCOMPATIBLE, plus the system's own status message appended). This is the same channel the Play Store uses — no FileProvider/MIME/OEM-resolver guesswork, and any future failure reports its true reason instead of a silent parse dialog.
  2) FileProvider + ACTION_VIEW kept as automatic fallback when createSession/openSession/commit throws (OEM session quirks).
  3) Gate 4 added — signer-identity pre-check: archive certs (GET_SIGNING_CERTIFICATES on API 28+, GET_SIGNATURES below) vs installed app certs; mismatch → "installed Pulse was signed with a different key — uninstall it once, then install this update" instead of firing the installer.
  4) Manifest gains apkUrlMirror; downloader tries the GitHub release asset first, and on network failure falls back to the raw-CDN mirror from a clean slate (never mixes byte streams; sha256 gate still enforced). auto-resume budget resets per URL.
  5) New UpdateState.Installing; UpdaterBanner/UpdaterDetail when() branches added ("Installing — confirm the system dialog" / "Handed to the system installer — confirm to finish"); tapping while Installing re-opens the installer with the cached verified bytes instead of re-downloading 14 MB.
  6) Crash fix: install() called canRequestPackageInstalls() unconditionally (API-26-only method → NoSuchMethodError on Android 5-7); now guarded by Build.VERSION.SDK_INT >= 26.
- Version bump: app/build.gradle.kts defaults → versionCode 7, versionName 0.1.6-native.
- PUBLISH plan: one push → android-ci + ios-ci → forensic gate on the new artifact (AXML versionCode=7, arsc, sig blocks, sha256) → Release v0.1.6-native via REST (full-SHA target_commitish, --http1.1) + asset Pulse-v0.1.6-native.apk → CDN: download/Pulse.apk replaced, update-manifest.json gains apkUrlMirror + versionCode 7 + new sha256 → push.

Stage Summary:
- v0.1.5 bytes are provably valid; the user's parse error is device-path, not APK — v0.1.6 closes every remaining device-path class: session-API install with exact status surfacing, signer pre-check, mirror download, API-21-25 crash guard
- Whatever happens on the user's device next, the update UI now TELLS US the system's real reason — no more blind guessing
- CDN manifest after publish: versionCode 7 / 0.1.6-native / apkUrl (release asset) + apkUrlMirror (raw CDN) / sha256 of shipped bytes
- RESULTS: android-ci GREEN first round (run 34424858351); artifact 13,984,863 bytes, sha256 39a4d96053284614607caf46c88346e5994f5b0951d53c32e8495799698d3096; forensic gate passed (AXML versionCode=7, versionName 0.1.6-native, package app.pulse.chat, minSdk 21, targetSdk 35, extractNativeLibs TRUE; ZIP clean; arsc STORED+aligned; v1 CERT.SF/CERT.RSA + v2/v3 block); Release v0.1.6-native (id 385945616) + asset Pulse-v0.1.6-native.apk (id 553968679, state uploaded); public release URL bytes sha256 == artifact EXACTLY; CDN manifest live with apkUrlMirror; mirror (raw CDN download/Pulse.apk) bytes sha256 == artifact EXACTLY. Task ID: W0-A
Agent: Android Wave 0 crew (W0-A)
Task: Wave 0 Android implementation

Work Log:
- Read W0-PLAN + W0-B sections of worklog.md and Task 2-a (Android ground truth), packages/protocol/src/contracts.ts end-to-end (CLIENT_EVENTS 22 / SERVER_EVENTS 27 / NOTIFY_EVENTS 11 + all payload types), web src/lib/pulse-outbox.ts (FIFO ≤50, stop-at-first-failure, temp→real swap, attempts bump) and .github/workflows/android-ci.yml (read-only)
- Found the working tree already carried a staged W0-A implementation from a prior session (uncommitted); this session audited EVERY in-scope file line-by-line against the binding scope, re-verified the full scope list (protocol registry, socket client, endpoints/manifest hook, SecureSessionStore, Room v4 + migration + DAOs, outbox engine + triggers + worker, drafts, stub removal, version bump, all 5 test targets), then fixed the real defects found:
  1) protocol/src/test/resources/pulse-relay/.gitignore was ALSO ignoring package-lock.json — trimmed to `node_modules/` only so the lockfile is committed and CI npm installs are deterministic
  2) SocketRoundTripTest could never pass against socket.io: java.net.http.HttpClient (JDK 21) sends `Upgrade: h2c` on cleartext by default and engine.io accepts the connection but returns ZERO bytes (verified with a node echo server: plain node http answers Java fine; engine.io-attached server swallows h2c-upgrade requests; curl fine). Fix: HttpClient.newBuilder().version(HTTP_1_1) — REQUIRED on CI JDK-21 runners too, or /notify + health probe stall
  3) Order(5) used a manual client.disconnect() — socket.io never auto-reconnects a deliberately-disconnected client ("io server disconnect"), so the reconnect re-join assertion dead-waited. Rebuilt as a REAL network blip: relay gains GET /kick?userId= which ABORTS the raw transport (ws.terminate() on websocket / socket.destroy() on polling — no engine.io CLOSE packet); client must auto-reconnect and the EVENT_CONNECT handler must re-emit join. Also replaced the repeat(30){return@repeat} health-probe loop (return@repeat only skips the iteration) with an early-exit while, and made Order(2) tolerant of the stale {alice}-only presence snapshot legitimately in flight across the drain (poll-until-both within deadline)
  4) data/build.gradle.kts: removed the still-unused androidx.work.runtime declaration from :data (grep-zero androidx.work imports in :data; :app declares and uses it for OutboxWorker)
